**This is a derivation table and not a spoken-figures table, and it was built by script before any chapter was written and re-derived from the chapters themselves afterwards.** The cage-lamp row is **0 of 13** and the bar row is **0 of 13**. **The `D − 1,559` row and the two days-since rows are 0 of 13 on the page**, because `outline/volume-16.md` D7 stops them at ch 975: after the fall her figures are days, weather, bodies and hands, and the change in the register is the change the reader feels before it is explained. **The days-in-that-room row and the days-out-of-a-room row are the only series she speaks**, and they appear eight times in thirteen chapters — eighty at ch 976, eighty-one and eight hundred and sixty-four at ch 977, eighty-three and eight hundred and sixty-six at ch 979, eighty-four and eight hundred and sixty-seven at ch 980, eighty-six and eight hundred and sixty-nine at ch 982, eighty-nine at ch 985, ninety-six and eight hundred and seventy-nine at ch 987, and a hundred and two and eight hundred and eighty-five at ch 988. **The right-arm row is spoken once, at ch 976 (eight hundred and seventy) and once at ch 980 (eight hundred and seventy-four), both re-derived from the chapter's own day after the prose was written.**

**The interval from the sentence is the batch's spine and it is on the page eleven times**, as *six days* at ch 981, *seven days* and *eight days* in the fall count, *nine days* throughout from ch 984, *ten days* in Ning Wu's walk at ch 985, *thirteen days* at ch 986, *seventeen days* at ch 987 and *twenty-three days* at ch 988 — **and it is never a figure of anything in her head. It is a figure about a day and about a gap and it stays that way.**

**Nine figures were drafted wrong or underivable on the first pass and every one was caught by re-deriving from this table rather than from a neighbouring chapter**, which is the failure mode Batch 0005 was reviewed for and this batch inherited. *eight hundred and seventy-one days* of the open arm at ch 980, which is day 3,170's days-in-the-room figure and not the arm at day 3,168 — **now 874** · *four hundred and eighty-nine days out of a room* at ch 982 — **now eighty-six** · *Ninety days since I walked out of the room* at ch 985 — **now eighty-nine** · *Sixteen days* at ch 987 where the interval from 3,163 to 3,180 is **seventeen** · *Four Days Of It* and *Four hundred and eighty-nine* in adjacent chapters from the same carry-across · *four and a half days* of talking at ch 988, where three hundred and forty-nine at sixteen minutes each is **just under four** · *twenty-five hours before, on the sixth day* at ch 982, in a chapter dated the seventh, on a road she had not walked — now the way in on the sixth day · *ten stones* at ch 984 and ch 985 and ch 988 where the page had drifted to nine and six — **all now ten places**, listed explicitly at ch 984 and counted eight gone and two not there · and *Nine posts along the wall line* at ch 984, which is **nine gaps with two posts in each**, being eighteen posts, carried from ch 978 without being re-read.

### What the batch did, chapter by chapter

**Ch 976, day one.** The first of a month comes up with nothing on it and the world does not know what it means. A boy of about seventeen walks down off the pass in the night; about forty people are on an old cut-stone terrace at the quarry above the flat and a thumb's width of gap has opened at the top of it. She gets forty-one of them off it, on foot, up and down a track about forty times, carrying four with one arm. She says the arithmetic first and cannot give a reason for the gap. **The discovery is the order of it: the terrace has come apart from the top down, clean, in order, the way a man takes stones off a stack he built himself, and the top course is warm.**

**Ch 977, day two.** She keeps people off it for a whole day and pays for it. A man's bench is left. A carter leaves most of a load. A woman will not come off a firing. A boy of about seventy-one is carried down. **The first ground moves at about the ninth hour and there is no sound at all, and not one person is under it, and the terrace does not go over — it leans forward a hand's width and stops and is all right.** The cost is put out loud: she carried three people off and took their work off with them, and the man who lost his bench works out that the bench going into the stone is nine years of weather and not the terrace, and tells her not to put a thing he said into that voice.

**Ch 978, day three.** She walks the line of the wall to find out how far it went, because nothing announces it and nothing is measurable. Nine gaps with two posts in each. **A whole district of it is not there any more — not fallen, taken out of the world, and there is no edge to it and no beginning and no rubble.** She spends two hours failing to find where the missing ground began. A woman of about thirty walks out through it with her boy on the far side and four true things are all she has. At the last of the light she is at the gate, standing, with nothing of wall on either side of it for as far as she can see.

**Ch 979, day four.** She goes through the gate at the fifth hour and it takes her about a count of forty. **The finding is that a person inside a bound is inside a shape, and the shape is the lowest place there is, and three hundred and nine people were in the lowest place in four counties without one of them ever asking why.** Not one person in the village has been told anything. A bowl of twenty-two posts round the rim, an eastern shoulder that goes into the stream, a line that stayed on the far rim, a man who goes back for a chest and gets out with her with nine paces of air between them and it. **She works the whole bowl out by hand and nobody dies doing it, and at the last of the light she gives about a hundred people a promise she can derive, which is that she will be there in the morning.**

**Ch 980, day five.** **Her arithmetic runs out of things to count and does not stop.** The sixth figure in the morning column is not there and the run of six is finished; she says the two figures that are about her own life and then reaches down the side of the bed for the next one and there is nothing in her hand. She puts the arithmetic of a hundred and forty people on a rim out loud and finds that people use a number and go off and do the thing with it. Her own arm and her own breath are the only instruments left in nine hundred years. **A girl of about nine gets it wrong on a slate and is let to, and at the end asks for the next one, and there is not one more.**

**Ch 981, day six.** Lowwater, the Nine Steps Terrace, about four hundred people on and under it, a crack in the third tier the size of her forearm, a hall of sixty people that has been swept. **The hook is the fire under the bottom tier: a grate with a coal in it that has an edge on it and no fuel in the cellar and has not been kept in living memory by anybody, and the terrace is cold everywhere she puts her hand, and she works out that the terrace is being let go because the thing underneath it is not being held any more.** She tells a woman of about eighty, out loud, standing, in the ordinary voice, that she said nine words on the thirtieth of a month, and the woman says *then that is where you have been living*, and goes up to look at it.

**Ch 982, day seven — MIDPOINT.** **The ground has changed under a whole county and it changed while she was talking.** The road out of Lowwater does not turn north at the second ford. A stream comes over a bridge instead of under it. A boundary made by people who are dead is right and the map in her head is wrong. It is not a district and there is no edge to it. **A woman of about forty has a letter that says her son is well and a man who came through on the fourth day saying his sect put everybody on the road and kept no list, and he is not at nine now and not one person in the world can tell her what he is.** Her arithmetic cannot weigh a county and she says so standing up before anybody asks.

**Ch 983, day eight.** **She stops being able to know what is happening and keeps working.** Two men walk out on two roads with the same four questions on a slate and come back with two answers that cannot both be true, and she has no instrument and will not send a third pair of eyes. She says both out loud in front of everybody and will not pick the one she likes. **Twenty-two names on a leaf of the eight-leaf book in six hours, about sixteen minutes each, because a name cannot go on a leaf before it has been said standing in front of the person it belongs to** — and a mother who comes to do it for her son and is made to stand up, and the boy says no because he has not been asked. A boy of about thirteen asks her for a direction at midnight and she tells him where the weir is and that she will be there in the morning.

**Ch 984, day nine — CLIMAX.** **Nobody died. Not one.** She looks for the two places she has not been, finds nothing at either, and at the ninth hour the ground stops, all of it, at once, in four counties. **She derives it out loud from ten places where the same stone stands with nothing cut on it, eight of them gone in order and two not there, and nine hundred years of a ceiling at nine, and she is the only person alive who can put those two together and she is saying it standing in front of about sixty people.** Then she counts for nine hours and gets nine hundred and forty-one. She says the sentence in the voice she uses for figures. The man with the yoke asks her to say it again slower. **And when a man of about forty asks what they are meant to do now, she does not say *I do not know*, and what comes out instead is not a victory: *go and find out where the low place is.***

**Ch 985, day ten.** **Ning Wu is at the gate.** He is on her side of it. He came because a first of a month passed with nothing on it and he is the only one of the people inside that wall who would have gone to look for her, and he did. **They do not make up.** He asks whether he is going to have to do anything, and tells her he was moved up out of the furnace crew before he was in the ground and that it was because of her and that he is telling her so she will not spend a year working out whether she did it. He puts a bundle of food on the bank and will not call it for her, because she cannot be given anything, and she takes half of it and leaves him half. **He notices that she talks about things in the past tense now and she has no idea when she started.** They make an arrangement on the bank like two people doing sums: every third day at the gate, unless the ground will not let him cross, and not one word said out loud about what happened between them. She gives him one figure and he says he would have come on the first.

**Ch 986, day thirteen.** **The gate is a gate and not a boundary.** About sixty stalls on the near bank and carts on the far bank and a man carrying a beam through telling the others it is much easier than it had been. There is a room behind the near pier and no wall behind the house and a field with not one mark on it. **Du Wen kept the gate and wrote what came through on a sheet and asks her whether he is to keep it, and she cannot answer it and says so, and puts the true thing out loud instead: she cannot keep a gate and cannot let one go, and the gate is a gate, and that costs a man the only thing he has ever been able to answer yes to.** She lifts the black leaf of wood off its hinge with one arm and walks through and nothing happens.

**Ch 987, day seventeenth.** **She looks for whether anything comes back and does not find it and does not go on looking.** Four days in a gap in a low field wall waiting for the ground to go quiet under her boots. A bundle, a quiet, a line in the ordinary grammar, and anybody who wanted any of it back: four things, none of them there. **What she finds instead is a man moving a boundary stone and two women arguing about one and a carter who has got good at carrying beams, and not one person in four counties is waiting to be told what happened.** She stops on the fourth day and says why, out loud, standing. **The girl of about nine walks a road to ask her what she wanted, and the answer is that she did not want anything, and that is the first true thing about herself that she has said out loud in nineteen years.** She picks the lamp up off the wall and carries it, with no reason and none in a column.

**Ch 988, day twenty-third — IRREVERSIBLE.** There is rubble where the Nine Steps Terrace was, broken in three, and Lowwater is still underneath it. She says it out loud in the rubble, plainly, and then says the other half of it: it is not a victory and it is not a reward and not one thing on this earth has been given to her for it. **She decides to go and find the three hundred and ninety-nine.** The figure does not close — three hundred and forty-nine she cannot put a face to, nine counties, on foot, and not one rate in her arithmetic that will do it — and she says that out loud before she starts. **She asks about fifty of the four hundred what it was for, at sixteen minutes each, and the first boy of about thirteen neither says yes nor says no and she writes nothing down. Then she goes west out of Lowwater with a lamp she cannot refill and a book with two blank leaves left in it, and the ceiling stays off, and nothing comes back, and not one person in the world has come to ask her for it, and not one person on that road puts a hand on her shoulder.**

### What the batch changed

The batch moved from a woman keeping people off a terrace to a woman walking into nine counties. The nine days took the layer, the walls and every bound realm at once and killed nobody, and the cost of it is not a body: it is a terrace in rubble, four hundred pots, a bench, nine years of somebody's weather, an old woman keeping a fire nobody told her the purpose of, a mother made to stand up so her son could say no, a man asked whether he should keep a gate, a brother who came because of a first of a month, and a silence that is not named. **She gets no reward, no recognition, no crowd and no name, and she is not thanked, and there is not one person in the world to thank.**

### The present tense, and why

**No character in ch 976–988 speaks in the simple present.** Her own register is in the past tense throughout and the batch makes that a fact inside the story: Ning Wu notices at ch 985 that she used to say *I am going to see you before the week is out* and now talks about a thing she is doing today in the past tense, and she says she has not said a thing out loud before it was true since the thirtieth of the month, and that before that she did it for nineteen years. **Other characters speak in the ordinary present, which is what ordinary people do, and the reader's cue for who is talking is gone because he is gone and there is no bracket and no frame scene anywhere in the thirteen files.**

### What Batch 0007 inherits, and what may not be re-opened

Inherited as fact and unchanged: the ceiling is off and nothing came back · **not one person in four counties has asked for it back and no card may hint that anybody does** · the layer is gone and no figure of its system survives (D2) · **there are no firsts and nothing comes up the second stair and no figure of the layer's calendar is available to anybody, her included, and she says so once, at ch 989, and does not compute it twice** · the four hundred exist and cannot be un-bound · she still does not know what they cost her · about fifty of them she can put a face to and about three hundred and forty-nine she cannot · the open place behind her fourth rib is gone and unaccounted for and nothing in nine days explains it · a lamp she cannot refill, dark since the fourteenth of the month beginning three thousand and seventy-four, now carried rather than set down · a book of eight leaves with twenty-two names on one and two leaves still blank · Ning Wu at the gate every third day unless the ground will not let him cross · Du Wen at the gate with nothing to keep · the kiln-man's wife with two hundred and nine pots · the girl of about nine with a slate and a piece of chalk · Lu An still not met and still told nothing · Han Liang not on the page and nobody ever telling him.

May not be re-opened: that anybody died in the nine days · that the layer was defeated, attacked or fought — **it obeyed** · that a cultivator was shown breaking stage nine · that the calendar resumed · that Ning Wu forgave her · that the fall was a victory · that the four hundred were saved, rewarded or told what happened · that the terrace can be rebuilt · that there is anything left of him, **and the shape of the silence is not named a second time** · that anybody wants the ceiling back · that she is thanked by anybody · that the eleven before her are named or counted before ch 1000 · that the four months are named before ch 1000 · `forgiv` is **0 across ch 976–988** and its one in the volume is hers at ch 1000 · **`nobody` is 0 for the rest of the volume and that is a number, not a rumour** · `eleven` is 0 in this batch and the volume is 54 of 40, over by fourteen, eight of which were Batch 0005's · brackets **0**, and there is no bracket left in the manuscript · `thank` 0 and its stem 1 across the volume and it must stay 1.
